# Backlog

The single source of truth for **what is built and what is not**. One row per
shippable feature. Read together with `MASTER_INDEX.md` at the start of every
MODE: NEXT session — these two files are the entire resume state.

`status`: `todo` | `doing` | `done` | `blocked` | `dropped`
`done` means code exists and is reachable. Not "documented", not "planned".

Seeded 2026-09-04 by MODE: RECONCILE against the existing code. `done` rows are
proven by a code path. `todo` rows below F-017 are epic-sized placeholders
flagged `needs-decision` — each is replaced by real per-feature rows when its
area is pulled in with MODE: INGEST (§6d).

`spec ref` is the **feature catalog id** (`F-105`), never a line range — line
numbers shift on the first catalog edit. Resolve it with:

```bash
python3 tools/spec.py F-105     # the spec block for one feature
python3 tools/spec.py --todo    # catalog features with no backlog row yet
```

Note the two id namespaces: rows `F-001`…`F-041` are this backlog's own ids from
RECONCILE. Catalog ids (`F-101`…`F-1550`) arrive in the `spec ref` column, and
ingested rows reuse the catalog id verbatim as their row id.

| F-0207  | Switch to another member: issue that member's session, revoke the caller's (`account_switched`)             | audit          | done    | F-0205 F-004                  | F-0207   | txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/account-switch/account-switch.service.ts, txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/auth/auth.service.ts                                                             | `POST /auth/accounts/switch` with `{userId}` and no credential. `audit` decides membership, `identity` performs the handover (`AuthService.switchSession`): one transaction revokes the outgoing row `account_switched` and writes the incoming one (audit invariant #7), then the outgoing Redis marker is dropped **before** the incoming one is written, so `AuthGuard` never sees two live sessions. Adds `SessionRevokedReason.account_switched` — needs a Prisma migration. Not in the group / another group / another tenant / deleted / suspended / no resolvable scope all answer `accountSwitch.notAMember`. **Changed 2026-09-06 by ADR-0015**: membership is read per scope, and the incoming session is stamped with that scope                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| F-0208  | Remove an account from the group, revoking that member's live sessions                                      | audit          | done    | F-0205                        | F-0208   | txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/account-switch/account-switch.service.ts, txnet-backend/bot-service/src/app/flows/accounts.flow.ts, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/\_components/AccountSwitcher.tsx | `POST /auth/accounts/remove`, on both surfaces. **From either side**, as the catalog asks: any member may remove any other, and may remove itself — a group whose founder alone could evict would leave the _added_ account with no way out, which is the lock-in the row exists to remove. The session revoke is deliberately **narrow** (ADR-0015): `SessionService.revokeSessionsForUserInScope`, not `revokeAllSessionsForUser`, so removing an account in a browser cannot sign it out of a chat where it is still a member of a different group. Adds `SessionRevokedReason.account_unlinked` and `Session.scopeKey`. A group is torn down at one remaining member                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

| F-018-ag | A platform host serves a gated reseller **only** its configuration console: while `onboarding` is on, the reseller's `subdomain` answers the console's paths and nothing an end user would use — the mirror of F-066-q's allowlist, keyed on the gate instead of the domain's `purpose` | tenant | done | F-018-l F-066-q | — | `auth-service/src/app/tenant/tenant.ts` `gatedConsoleServesPath` + `CONSOLE_PATHS`/`END_USER_PATHS`; `tenant.guard.ts` refusal (4), keyed on `tenant:status:<surface tenant>`; `surfaceDomainType` through `tenant-resolver.service.ts` + `tenant-cache.service.ts`; `onboarding-surface.spec.ts` (16) | D-01 ("no platform domain or subdomain is ever served to an end user", every plan) is what F-018-l enforces, and it enforces it by **capability**: `register` / `sell` / `endUserDeposit` / `subscriptionLink` are closed. What is still open on a platform host is `signIn` / `read` / `account`, which cannot be closed by capability without locking the reseller's own staff out of the console (`tenant/open-questions.md` 2026-09-19). The right seam is the one F-066-q already built: resolution answers `surfacePurpose` from the surface, and a purpose carries a path allowlist. This row adds a third input — the gate — so a gated reseller's platform subdomain serves the console's paths only. **Why it matters to the user (2026-09-19): the platform's main domain must never be filtered because a reseller parked its end users on it.** Panel-web needs the mirror of the same rule (the open question of 2026-09-09). **Done 2026-09-19** (contract v33): the allowlist gained the gate as a third input — a `panel` `subdomain` of a gated reseller serves `gatedConsoleServesPath` only, the neutral 404 rather than the gate's 403, and the gate read is the *surface* tenant's (`brand?.id ?? id`, ADR-0059). The platform's own domain is never filtered because the platform owner is never gated. A path not on the list is closed, so a route added later is refused on a gated reseller's platform host until it is classified. `panel-web`'s mirror is still the open question of 2026-09-09 |

| F-031-a | The worker deployable, and the tick                                                                         | automation     | done    | —                             | —        | txnet-backend/shared-core/src/lib/automation/schedule.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/automation/tick.publisher.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/automation/tick.consumer.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/automation/worker-registry.service.ts | Shipped 2026-09-09. ADR-0027's substrate: `worker-service`, a Nx app that serves **no** HTTP (`createApplicationContext`, no port, no Traefik label — an HTTP surface would give back both failures the ADR names). automation contract **v3 -> v4**; invariants #1-#3 stop being `planned service layer` and name a call site. A job registers itself as a `bot_worker` on boot and boot never writes `isActive`, so a redeploy cannot switch a worker an admin switched off back on. The tick asks about the *interval since the last tick*, not the instant, so a late timer drops nothing; it resets on boot, so a week of downtime does not fire a week of occurrences at once. New deps: `amqplib`, `cron`. `schedule.spec.ts` 17/17 (moved to `shared-core` by F-031-b); the timezone case caught a real bug — `CronTime`'s constructor timezone is ignored by `getNextDateFrom`, so every `timezone` column would have been the process's local zone. **Not verified: a full process boot** — the dev database has never had `scripts/db-login-roles.sh` run, so `DATABASE_APP_URL`'s role does not exist there (F-066-m-a's one-time step). The broker half was proved against the live RabbitMQ: durable topic exchange, durable queue, `automation.tick.#` binding, persistent publish, JSON round trip. |
| F-031-b | An admin can register a schedule and flip a worker off                                                      | automation     | done    | F-031-a                       | —        | txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/automation/worker-admin.controller.ts, txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/automation/worker-admin.service.ts, txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/automation/manual-tick.publisher.ts, txnet-backend/shared-core/src/lib/automation/schedule.ts | Shipped 2026-09-09. Five `/admin/workers` routes behind `worker.manage`; automation contract **v4 -> v5**, auth-api **v11 -> v12** (both additive). Invariant #2 is now enforced where a schedule is *typed*, by the same `scheduleShapeError` the tick publisher declines on — which is why `schedule.ts` moved to `@txnet-backend/shared-core`: an Nx app cannot import an Nx app, and the writer and the runner disagreeing about a shape is exactly what the invariant forbids. `run now` bypasses the schedule and not `isActive`, so invariant #1 survives a manual trigger. `auth-service` becomes a broker *producer* for this one message; `RABBITMQ_URL` is optional here and the connection is lazy, so a broker that is down fails that route and not the boot. Nothing is deleted — a schedule is switched off. `npm test` 778/778, `shared-core` 17/17, `tsc --noEmit` clean on both the unit and the e2e projects. `worker-admin.e2e.spec.ts` written and **not run** (the tier is never run unasked); it covers the guards and the envelope only — the authorised half needs a `worker.manage` role and a `bot_worker` row, and this suite seeds neither. **Left open, on purpose:** invariant #3 still has no `*.int.spec.ts` (it needs a RabbitMQ fixture this repo does not have); `data-model.md` did not get the `bot_schedule` write path (§11's 3-file cap bound, §0 puts data-model last); `automation/contract.md` is at 231 lines, so the worker half is the natural `contract.<topic>.md` split next time it grows. Not verified: a live boot of either process. **Correction 2026-09-10 (found by e2e, F-067-j's session):** these routes were never reachable — `AutomationModule` declares the controller but imported nothing providing `AuthGuard`'s dependencies, so the service did not boot at all. Fixed with `forwardRef` on both sides; see `auth-api/open-questions.md`. The row stays `done` because the routes now answer, but `done` meant less than it claimed for four rows. |
| F-031-c | The first real job: `destroyExpiredVersions` | automation | done | F-031-a | — | txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/jobs/vault-retention.job.ts, txnet-backend/tenant-service/src/app/vault/vault-internal.controller.ts | Shipped 2026-09-09. `vault_credential_retention` — `worker-service`'s first job that does real work, and what closes ADR-0026 rule 4: a superseded credential version was verifiable for its grace window and then kept for ever, because `destroyExpiredVersions` had no caller. **The seam, decided:** an internal HTTP route (`POST /api/internal/vault/destroy-expired`, behind `ServiceOnlyGuard`, `@TenantAgnostic`), not a library move — moving the vault would drag its Prisma models, KEK and audit trail across an app boundary for one caller, and `contract.vault.md` puts the obligation to *schedule* on `automation`, not the code. `worker-service` gains `AUTH_API_BASE_URL` / `SERVICE_AUTH_TOKEN` / `AUTH_API_TIMEOUT_MS`, all **optional and read per run**: a job whose seam is unconfigured fails its own run into `bot_execution_log` while every other job keeps running. The spec states the one thing that would rot silently — a sweep must never report success without a count from auth-api, since an unset token, a guard's 404 and an unrecognised shape all look like 'nothing was due'. `worker-service` 7/7, `npm test` 778/778, `tsc --noEmit` clean on all four projects. One e2e case added to `contract.e2e.spec.ts` (**not run** — the tier is never run unasked); it covers the guard's 404 only, because giving the harness a `SERVICE_AUTH_TOKEN` is a `support/**` change and would make every e2e run a six-file run. **§11's 3-file write cap:** `automation/contract.md` hit §10's 250-line hard fail, so the worker half moved to `contract.worker.md` — the split F-031-b's note predicted. Counted as one contract, since the move is mechanical and the alternative was a failing checker. **Left open, on purpose:** invariant #3 still has no `*.int.spec.ts`; the heartbeat job stays until a second real job exists; no schedule row is seeded, so the job runs only when an admin gives it one or presses `run now`. Not verified: a live boot of either process. |

| F-311 | Reseller management panel inside the bot | bot-app | dropped | F-303-b F-061-i F-066-w1 | F-311 |  | **Split 2026-09-20 into F-311-a..d** (user). Two findings forced it. (1) The dependency `F-018` was split on 2026-09-17 and is `dropped`, so this row pointed at a row nobody can finish; what it stood for is `F-061-i` (the owner is signed in in their own reseller's bot chat) + `F-066-w1` (`ResellerAccess`). (2) Of the spec's four verbs, **none of the data sides exist**: `where.py` finds no surface for a reseller reading or blocking its own users, none for its revenue, and grants are the `entitlement` unit, still `draft` (schema only, F-026-b). The bot panel was never the missing part — the APIs under it were. Each is its own row now, and the bot screen (F-311-c) sits on top |
| F-311-a | A reseller reads and blocks its own users: `GET /api/auth/tenants/:tenantId/users` (page, search by phone/username/name) and block / unblock one, admitted by `F-066-w1` (owner, a staff seat with `tenant.manage`, or platform staff) and run in that reseller's scope — a reseller sees only its own tenant's users, never the platform's | identity | done | F-066-w1 F-018-ad | F-311 | txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/auth/users/reseller-users.service.ts, reseller-users.controller.ts, reseller-users.schema.ts, reseller-users.spec.ts | Split off F-311 2026-09-20 (user). The data side of "create user, block" — built once here so the bot (F-311-c) and a future panel page share it, the way F-066-w3 serves F-066-w4. Creation is deliberately out: a reseller's user is created by registering on that reseller's domain (F-061-d), not by an admin typing one in — raise it as its own row if the spec really means an admin-made account. Done 2026-09-20: list/block/unblock under `ResellerAccess`, no query naming a `tenantId` — the scope the door opens is the whole filter. `banned` outranks a block, so a reseller neither sets nor lifts the platform's ban; a block revokes every session `admin_ban` after the tx commits and audits `user_ban` under the reseller. Shapes `interfaces/auth-api/contract.reseller-users.md`, semantics `domains/identity/contract.reseller-users.md` |
| F-311-b | A reseller's own revenue, as a figure it can be shown: totals over a period for one tenant from the existing payment + wallet ledgers, admitted by `F-066-w1` and scoped to that reseller | billing | done | F-066-w1 | F-311 | txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/revenue/reseller-revenue.service.ts, reseller-revenue.controller.ts, reseller-revenue.schema.ts, revenue.module.ts, reseller-revenue.spec.ts | Split off F-311 2026-09-20 (user). **The `needs-decision` was answered first, by the user, and is written once in ADR-0067** — three ledgers here can each be called revenue and no two agree. `GET /api/billing/tenants/:tenantId/revenue?from=&to=`, `ResellerAccess` the only door (`read`, so a suspended reseller still sees it), the scope the whole filter — no query names a `tenantId`, and both tables are strict under RLS *and* in `TENANT_SCOPED_MODELS`. **Two figures, neither folded into the other**: `sales` (wallet debits with a sale reason) and `topUps` (settled payments, `billingTenantId: null` so the reseller's own payment to the platform is not its income). **Gross** — the platform's cut lives on `tenant_billing_transaction` on a different clock, so netting it would be a subtraction no row backs. A sale is classified by an exhaustive `IS_SALE` over `WalletReasonType`, so a new reason does not compile until somebody judges it; `wallet_transfer_out` is out because two users could otherwise manufacture revenue in a loop, `sub_account_charge` because the consumption out of that wallet is already counted. `settlement` (F-096-e) was not reused, re-scoped or extended. **Known and deliberate: `sales.total` is `0.00` until `entitlement` is built** — nothing writes `traffic_consumption` (F-026-b, the same gap that blocks F-311-d). `topUps` is real today, and F-311-c must show it as what it is rather than a zero labelled revenue. 7 cases in `reseller-revenue.spec.ts`, mutation-checked twice: widening `IS_SALE` turns one red, dropping the door turns all seven red. New bucket `RESELLER_REVENUE_READ` — `shared-core`'s snapshot caught it, updated deliberately. Shapes and rules `domains/billing/contract.revenue.md` (contract v19); surface `billing-api-reseller-revenue` |

The flow only; every fact it shows belongs to another unit, as `bot-app/contract.md` "the decision belongs to" requires. This is the row F-312, F-313-b and F-1531 actually wait on. **F-311-e was split out of it on 2026-09-20** (user, راه ۱): the bot cannot tell a reseller's owner from one of its customers, so it had nothing to gate the menu row on — the owner signs in in their own platform tenant (ADR-0059 (6)) and neither `/auth/me` nor the session names the reseller they own. Build the menu row and the write buttons on that verdict, never on a rule of the bot's own. Done 2026-09-20: the menu row is drawn on the door's `canRead` for **the bot's** tenant (`ctx.integration.tenantId`, F-320) and nothing else — a refusal, an unreachable tenant-service and an unset `TENANT_API_BASE_URL` are all silently no row, because this same bot serves the reseller's customers. Nothing cached (ADR-0033): one verdict per menu render, one more for any screen offering a write, and `canWrite` is asked separately so a **suspended** reseller reads its customers and sees no block button. Typing on the list is the search box a chat lacks; under three characters is refused here without a call. The page rides on `rpage:<n>` and only the search is kept, so an older keyboard cannot page a newer screen; a customer is opened by re-listing the remembered page and finding the row, never from the button's id — `auth-api` grew no read-one-user route for this. Block asks on its own screen and the confirming tap must name the same person the state does (the `drop:` pattern); unblock is one tap. Revenue shows billing's two figures side by side and never added, with `sales` at `0.00` until `entitlement` lands (F-311-b's own note). 20 cases in `reseller.flow.spec.ts` + 2 in `router.spec.ts`, mutation-checked: admitting everyone turns the two menu-row cases red, dropping the target check turns the stale-keyboard case red. New env var `TENANT_API_BASE_URL` — the gate, never tenant-service directly, the `BILLING_API_BASE_URL` precedent. The three segments are compositions of notification's own audience filter (all / active / joined in the last 30 days) and the button carries the **key**, never the filter — a filter that arrived as input is one a caller can write. The count is `POST audience/count` and the screen says *about*, for the contract's own reason. Part 1 of 2 of F-066-m. `tenant-context` contract **v4**, tenant invariant **13** — the unit's first enforcer that is not code. `withTenant` binds `app.tenant_id` in each query's own transaction and RLS answers it; the service connects as `DATABASE_APP_URL`, NOBYPASSRLS and owner of nothing, with **no fallback** to `DATABASE_URL` (a fallback leaves every policy inert and looks fine). Needs a one-time `./scripts/db-login-roles.sh` per database — see `docs/operations/migrations.md`. `npm test` 739/739, `tsc` clean, and eight isolation properties proved by hand against Postgres 18 (`tenant/invariants.md` — the e2e tier uses `prisma db push`, so it has no policies to test). |
| F-066-m-b | Every remaining `tenantId` table, and the cross-tenant pool that retires `runAcrossTenants` | tenant | done | F-066-m-a | F-1202 | txnet-backend/prisma/domains/migrations/20260909001500_row_level_security_all_tables/migration.sql, txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/prisma/cross-tenant-prisma.service.ts | All three shipped: RLS on the remaining 23 `tenantId` tables in `20260909001500_row_level_security_all_tables` (three policy shapes — that file says which table has which and why); `CrossTenantPrismaService` on `DATABASE_CROSS_TENANT_URL`, a role whose policy is `USING (true)` and never `BYPASSRLS`; all four readers moved onto it, so `runAcrossTenants` is deprecated with no callers left (`tenant-context/contract.md` v5 rule 4, tenant invariant 13). Two decisions taken here: the directory's two cross-tenant **writes** go through the pool as well (the catalog names only reads — contract rule 4 says why), and `tenant.tenant` is left unpolicied because it has no `tenantId` column, so its rule is `id = current_tenant_id()` and belongs to a row of its own. | F-067-a | OTP delivery leaves the request path | identity | done | F-067-d F-067-f F-031-a | — | txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/auth/otp/otp.service.ts, txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/auth/otp/otp-delivery.store.ts, txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/auth/otp/otp-delivery.publisher.ts, txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/auth/otp/otp-internal.controller.ts, txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/automation/broker.publisher.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/otp/otp-delivery.consumer.ts | Opened 2026-09-10 from an architecture review, on the user's call. `issueOtp` calls `sender.send` — an SMS / Telegram / Bale HTTP round trip — inside the `login` and `register` request **and** inside the Redis lock it takes at `otp.service.ts:42` and releases at `:88`. A slow provider therefore holds a lock and an event-loop slot on the process that also answers `/auth/login`, which is the exact failure ADR-0027 moved background work out of `auth-service` for; OTP was simply never covered by it. Only the send is published — the draw, the argon2 hash, the Redis save, the cooldown and the `otp_code` row stay in the request, because they are what makes the code verifiable. **Answered 2026-09-10 (D-15, revised the same day):** the route answers **202**, and the delivery result is pushed to the user's WebSocket (F-067-h), not polled. The Redis status stays, as the fallback a client that reconnects or never opened a socket reads once. So a send that fails after the 202 reaches the user instead of only a log line. **Split 2026-09-10 (§6d.5), on the user's sequencing call — queues first, sockets after:** this row ships the queue half only. The client reads the Redis delivery status; no socket is involved and F-067-i is *not* a dependency. The push half is **F-067-j**, and nothing built here is thrown away — D-15 keeps that status as the fallback a reconnecting client reads for ever. **Assumption to confirm before F-067-j:** at `login` / `register` there is no session, so the 202 carries a random channel name and a token whose TTL matches the OTP's, and that channel carries only `sent` / `failed` — never the code. Nothing else can scope a pre-login socket. Two consequences carried by that answer: the route can no longer return `otp.smsSendFailed`, so this is an auth-api contract break and a version bump (§5.4); and the plaintext code does not ride the queue — the message carries the Redis key the code was already saved under. → becomes an ADR when this is built. **Shipped 2026-09-10.** The three issuing routes answer **202** with a `deliveryId` and `POST /auth/otp/delivery/status` says what became of the send; `interfaces/auth-api/contract.md` v13 has the wire, `domains/identity/contract.md` v11 the why. **The note's mechanism could not be built as written**: the Redis key it said the message would carry holds an argon2id hash, not the code, so the draw moved to the sender instead of the plaintext moving to Redis — invariant #2 stands unamended. The send runs in `auth-service` over the internal seam, triggered by a `worker-service` consumer, which is the answer `vault_credential_retention` already gave to the same question (F-031-c). D-15's status store is built and is F-067-j's fallback. |
| F-067-b | The bot webhook acks and enqueues instead of running the flow | bot-app | done | F-067-d F-067-f | — | txnet-backend/shared-core/src/lib/automation/bot-update.ts, txnet-backend/bot-service/src/app/webhook/bot-update.publisher.ts, txnet-backend/bot-service/src/app/webhook/bot-dispatch.controller.ts, txnet-backend/bot-service/src/app/webhook/webhook.controller.ts, txnet-backend/bot-service/src/app/common/service-only.guard.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/bot/bot-update.consumer.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/broker/broker.service.ts | Opened 2026-09-10 from an architecture review, on the user's call. `webhook.controller.ts` runs the whole conversation — `BotDispatcher`, the `auth-api` call inside it, and the `sendMessage` back to the platform — before returning 200. Telegram times out and redelivers, so at scale one slow tenant occupies the shared `bot-service` and earns duplicate updates for everyone on it. The route should verify path and secret exactly as it does now, publish the normalised `ChatContext`, and return 200. **Answered 2026-09-10 (D-16):** a **routing key per chat over a fixed queue set** — `hash(chatId)` picks the queue, each queue has one consumer at `prefetch: 1`. Order within a chat is then a property of the topology rather than of a lock, and parallelism scales with the number of queues. A single shared queue with a Redis lock per `chatId` was rejected: a locked-out update has to go back on the queue, which is the ordering problem again with more moving parts. The rule that the answer is always 200 once path and secret are good survives either way, and gets easier. **Shipped 2026-09-10.** The webhook verifies, publishes to `bot.update.<slot>` and answers 200; `worker-service` consumes one queue per slot at `prefetch: 1` and runs the flow through `POST /api/internal/bots/dispatch`. The wire and the reasoning are in `interfaces/bot-app/contract.webhook.md` (contract v11). **The rule the note expected to survive did not**: an unconfirmed publish now answers 5xx on purpose, because the platform's redelivery is this path's only recovery — the message carries the webhook path, never the integration, so no tenant id or credential ref rides the queue. `automation`'s `contract.worker.md` lags one session on the queue set (§11's 3-file cap). |
| F-067-c | The outbox table and the relay worker | automation | done | F-067-d F-067-g | — | txnet-backend/prisma/domains/automation.prisma, txnet-backend/prisma/domains/migrations/20260910001000_automation_outbox, txnet-backend/shared-core/src/lib/automation/outbox.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/jobs/outbox-relay.job.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/broker/broker.service.ts, dev-docker/monitoring/config-dev/postgres-queries.yaml, dev-docker/monitoring/config-dev/automation.rules.yml | Opened 2026-09-10 from an architecture review, on the user's call. ADR-0021 decided a transactional outbox relayed to RabbitMQ on 2026-09-09 and **none of it was built**: there is no `outbox` model in `prisma/domains/`, and `worker-service` registers only `worker_heartbeat` and `vault_credential_retention`. Nothing breaks today because every producing domain (`billing`, `network`, `notification`, `ai`) is `draft` — which is also why this is cheap now and expensive on the day the first payment lands. Scope: the table (event id, aggregate, type, payload, `publishedAt`), the relay job that drains it, and the consumer-side idempotency key ADR-0021 requires. **Answered 2026-09-10 (D-14):** it lives **inside `automation`** — the table in `prisma/domains/`, the relay as a third job in `worker-service` beside `worker_heartbeat` and `vault_credential_retention`. No new unit. ADR-0021 calls the outbox a new unit and the status-quo boundary won instead, which §6.6 makes a note rather than an ADR. Splitting it out later stays cheap while it is one table and one job. **Carries its own monitoring (F-067-g, 2026-09-10):** the age of the oldest unpublished row is the one number F-067-g could not ship, because this is the row that creates the table. Add the gauge and its alert rule to `dev-docker/monitoring/config-dev/automation.rules.yml` here. Note that it needs a metrics endpoint the repo does not have — every existing rule reads off the broker, and Postgres-side numbers have no scrape target yet. **Shipped 2026-09-10.** `automation.outbox_event` plus `outbox_relay`, a third `worker-service` job that claims rows `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and stamps `publishedAt` only after a confirmed, routable publish; the why is `domains/automation/contract.outbox.md` (a §10 split — `contract.worker.md` was at 250 lines). The scrape target the note wanted is `postgres-exporter` running one query, not a `/metrics` on `worker-service` — ADR-0027 makes that process one that serves no requests, and three rules now read the gauge. **Two things the scope named are not built**: the consumer-side idempotency store is a wire guarantee only (the event id rides as `messageId`; there is no consumer to hold a `processed_event` table for), and the relay needs an `always_on` `bot_schedule` like every other job. |
| F-067-d | A dead-letter path, before anything but a tick rides the broker | automation | done | F-031-a | — | txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/broker/broker.service.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/automation/dead-letter.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/automation/dead-letter.drain.ts, txnet-backend/auth-service/src/app/automation/worker-admin.controller.ts, txnet-backend/prisma/domains/migrations/20260910000000_automation_dead_letter | Opened 2026-09-10 from an architecture review, on the user's call. `BrokerService.consumeTicks` nacks a failed handler with `requeue: false`, and no dead-letter exchange is declared on the queue, so the message is destroyed. `contract.worker.md` argues that correctly **for a tick** — a tick recurs on the next interval and the failure is a `bot_execution_log` row with `status = failed` — and the argument does not survive the first message that does not recur. An OTP, an outbox event or a bot update dropped this way is gone with no record anywhere. Needs a DLX and dead-letter queue, a redelivery/attempt count on the message, and one place an operator sees what landed there. Every other row in this series depends on it, which is why it is first. · **Shipped 2026-09-10.** The queue carries `x-dead-letter-exchange`, so every rejection — a failed handler, a body that is not JSON, and now a tick the gate gave up on — is moved rather than destroyed; `DeadLetterDrain` records each one in `automation.dead_letter` and `GET /admin/workers/dead-letters` reads them. Why the queue is renamed `.v2`, why the reason is derived from the message and why the drain requeues on a write failure are in `domains/automation/contract.worker.md`; the rule is invariant #9. Re-driving a dead message is deliberately not built — it is a decision about ordering and idempotency, not a button. |

A queue per tenant — what F-066-p's note calls the real replacement — was rejected as the heavier answer for now, though F-067-b's per-chat queue set may make it cheap later. **Shipped 2026-09-10.** The count is a sorted set of expiring leases in Redis, not a counter — `INCR`/`DECR` lets a replica killed mid-run shrink a tenant's budget for ever, and a lease expires at `AUTOMATION_RUN_TIMEOUT_MS`, the deadline that already writes the run row off. `domains/automation/contract.tenant-cap.md` (a §10 split — `contract.worker.md` hit 274 lines) says why, invariant #8 says what. **One thing the answer did not name:** what a Redis outage does. | F-067-i | A result computed in a worker reaches the socket that is holding the user | realtime | done | F-067-h F-067-d | — | txnet-backend/shared-core/src/lib/realtime/fanout.ts, txnet-backend/gateway-service/src/app/realtime/fanout.ts, txnet-backend/gateway-service/src/app/realtime/connection.registry.ts, txnet-backend/gateway-service/src/app/redis/redis.service.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/realtime/realtime.publisher.ts | Opened 2026-09-10 on the user's call. The process that finishes the work is `worker-service`; the process holding the user's socket is a gateway replica, and it is not the same process and usually not the same machine. So a result needs a fan-out: the worker publishes to a channel, and the gateway replica that holds that connection — and only it — delivers. Without this the socket works in dev with one replica and silently stops working the day there are two. Also decides what a message that arrives while nobody is connected does: dropped, with the Redis state as the record a reconnecting client reads (F-067-a), rather than an unbounded per-user buffer. First consumer is the OTP result; provisioning, payment confirmation and F-034's live chat are the same shape. **Shipped 2026-09-10.** The transport is **Redis pub/sub, not the broker**: a realtime event is at-most-once and dropped when nobody is connected, which is the opposite of everything RabbitMQ gives it — a durable queue per replica to declare and tear down, and a dead-letter path (F-067-d) preserving events for people who closed a laptop. Fan-out is **per channel**, so a replica subscribes to exactly what its own sockets hold and Redis routes; `RealtimeFanout.reconcile` keeps that set in step with `ConnectionRegistry` and reads the registry rather than the event that woke it, because a subscribe and an unsubscribe racing on one channel otherwise settle on whichever round trip finished last. Why, and the two-audience split, are in `platform/realtime/contract.fanout.md` (a §10 split — `contract.md` was at 205 lines). **The one thing that breaks silently, and what the spec pins:** ioredis does not apply `keyPrefix` to `PUBLISH`/`SUBSCRIBE`, because Redis does not count a channel as a key — so the prefix is applied by hand at both ends and a publisher that forgets it succeeds into a channel nobody hears. **No producer yet** — the seam is `RealtimePublisher` in `worker-service` and F-067-j is its first caller. `npm test` 802/802. |

It is authenticated by `forward-auth` like every other request, so the credential is the access token — which `PanelSessionContext` trades the refresh cookie for **once per page load**, and refresh rotates (F-0209). So the socket's credential and the page's can disagree, and that seam, not the frames, is what this row is about. `gateway-service` re-reads `session:<id>` every `REALTIME_SESSION_RECHECK_MS` and closes with **4401** when the marker is gone; the panel must route that into its own no-session redirect and not into a reconnect loop. It also has to decide where the provider sits relative to the account switcher: a switch changes *who* the socket is, so it is a close-and-reopen, not a resubscribe. **There is no producer for a signed-in channel yet** — F-067-i's fan-out has exactly one caller (the OTP result, on an anonymous channel), and F-034's live chat is the first real consumer. So this row's proof is a socket that survives navigation and a switch, not anything a user can see. **Worth asking before starting it**: whether it is built here or as the first half of F-034, which is the row that would give it something to carry. **Done 2026-09-10** on the user's call, which settles the row's own question: built here, not as the first half of F-034. `PanelRealtimeContext` sits at the panel layout inside `PanelSessionProvider`, so one connection outlives every screen, and `usePanelRealtime()` is how F-034 will reach it. The rules are in `panel-web/contract.realtime.md` (v12). The switcher needed nothing: it already reloads the document, and the socket is keyed to the current `userId` so that stays true whatever the switcher does later. One thing the row did not anticipate: `useRouter()` returns a fresh object per render, so listing it in the effect's deps tore the socket down and rebuilt it on every render — the redirect goes through a ref and the effect is keyed to the account alone. |

| F-096-a | Schema: a gateway grant (which tenant may use which gateway, granted by the platform owner), `payment_transaction.grantId`, a settlement ledger of what a granted gateway collected, and a recorded payout with its proof attachment | billing | done | F-092-d | — | txnet-backend/prisma/domains/billing.prisma, txnet-backend/prisma/domains/tenant.prisma, txnet-backend/prisma/domains/migrations/20260912000200_gateway_grant_and_settlement/migration.sql, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/gateway-grant-schema.int.spec.ts, docs/domains/billing/data-model.md | Shipped 2026-09-12; the rules are `domains/billing/data-model.md` "Migration notes", contract v10, and **ADR-0041** is the why. **Three tables, and they are three for a reason.** `payment_gateway_grant` is who may use whose gateway — a record and not a setting, withdrawn and never deleted, because a payment taken under it keeps pointing at it. `gateway_settlement_entry` is the debt, one append-only row per payment. `gateway_settlement_payout` is the repayment, recorded by a person with their proof. What is owed is the first minus the second and there is **no balance column**: ADR-0041 §5 says the ledger and not a remembered total says what is outstanding, and a cached total is a second memory. **The constraints are the content of this row**, each one a way the platform could otherwise owe the wrong tenant the wrong money: exactly one gateway per grant (a CHECK, like `payment_transaction`'s); **one *live* grant per (tenant, gateway)** by two partial unique indexes `WHERE "isActive"`, so granting → withdrawing → granting again is ordinary while two live rows disagreeing is impossible; a withdrawal that is complete (inactive rows say when and by whom, live rows say neither); **one accrual per payment** by unique key, because the transaction that will write it is reached by a retried callback *and* by F-092-l's sweep; and amount signs — an accrual `>= 0` because a fee can eat a whole payment, a payout `> 0`. **`tenantId` means the *borrowing* tenant** on all three, and carries that plain name rather than `granteeTenantId` because it is the column RLS and `withTenant` key on — a grant is exactly the borrower's to see. RLS is shape A (strict) on all three. **Reading is isolated; writing is not restricted to the platform owner** — the admin surface has no tenant of its own yet, the same gap `admin_audit_log` has, and F-096-e is where that is faced. Proved against a real Postgres built from the committed migration history: `gateway-grant-schema.int.spec.ts`, 14 cases, and two of them were red first for the right reason (a partial unique index reports the columns it is on, not its own name). Billing int 58 whole, billing unit 177, `npm test` 862 green, tsc clean over 16 tsconfigs after `prisma generate`. **Corrected on the way past:** billing invariant 9 still said "planned reconciliation worker"; F-092-l built it yesterday-in-this-session, so it now names `DepositReconciliationService` and the spec that holds it. Invariant 13 is new and is this row's. Schema only, as the row says — no service reads a grant yet, and the `grantId` column is nullable with NULL the ordinary case. |
| F-096-b | The list and the quote honour a grant: a tenant is offered the gateways granted to it beside its own, and a withdrawn or deactivated gateway disappears from both at once | billing | done | F-096-a F-092-u | — | txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-pricing.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-quote.service.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-start.service.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-gateways.int.spec.ts, docs/domains/billing/contract.deposit.md | Shipped 2026-09-12; the rules are `domains/billing/contract.deposit.md` "A gateway somebody else owns", contract v11. **The architectural call this row actually needed, made and written down:** a granted `tenant_gateway_config` row belongs to the *lender*, and that table's RLS is strict — on the borrower's connection it answers nothing, so a granted gateway would be invisible rather than offered. The read therefore runs on `CrossTenantPrismaService`, **bounded to the ids the borrower's own grants named** and selecting `GATEWAY_COLUMNS` only. The alternative was a wider RLS policy on that table — visible to every query from every service for ever, including the deprecated `*Encrypted` columns — against one bounded read in one function; ADR-0040 made the same call for a shared coupon's counters. This is the second reader of that pool in the service and the audit `grep` still lists them all. **F-092-u was the trap.** The vault filter asked the *caller's* vault for every row, and a granted gateway's merchant id is its owner's (D-26), so without also fixing that this row would have shipped as a visible no-op: every granted gateway dropped from the list, silently. `listGateways` now reads one vault listing per distinct owning tenant, and both refs (quote and start) name `ownerTenantId` — which is the data half of F-096-c. `selectGateway`/`selectableGateways` answer a `GatewayOffer` (`grantId`, `ownerTenantId`) and take the second pool; the four call sites were listed before the signature moved (§6.2b) and are all in this folder. `start` writes `grantId` on the payment, at start and not at settlement, because a grant can be withdrawn in between and what the platform owes is decided by the grant the payment was **made** under. **ADR-0041 §6 is proved, not assumed:** a withdrawn grant, a lender deactivating its gateway and an unverified row each remove it from the list *and* the quote at the same moment — six new cases in `deposit-gateways.int.spec.ts` against a real Postgres, including that a grant to one tenant gives nothing to another. Billing int 64 whole (16 in that file), billing unit 177, `npm test` 862 green, tsc clean over 16 tsconfigs. Unit fixtures gained a `paymentGatewayGrant` delegate and a per-owner `configuredLabels`, the second deliberately: a fake that answered one set for everybody would hide exactly the bug above. **Not covered, by design:** charging one. The credential crossing and its audit rule are F-096-c's, the debt is F-096-d's, and nothing creates or surfaces a grant yet (F-096-e) — a grant row is written by hand until then. |
| F-096-c | A granted gateway is charged with its owner's merchant id: `billing` reads a credential along a grant, audited, and nowhere else | billing | done | F-096-b | — | txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/gateway/granted-vault-access.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/gateway/granted-vault-access.spec.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/gateway/gateway-merchant.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/gateway/tenant-bound-vault-db.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/gateway/gateway-merchant.int.spec.ts, docs/domains/tenant/contract.vault.md | Shipped 2026-09-12; the rules are `domains/tenant/contract.vault.md` "The one crossing" (that unit v8), billing contract v12. **A narrow path, as the row asked, and not a second pool.** `tenantBoundVaultDb` still binds every vault query to the request's tenant; `GrantedVaultAccess.along(...)` overrides the bind target for **the duration of one call** and nothing else can open it. Giving the vault the cross-tenant connection would have let every vault read in this service reach every tenant's credentials, for ever, to serve one case — this is revocable by deleting a row. **The grant is proved, never accepted.** It is read on the *application* pool inside the borrower's own scope, so the RLS policy on `payment_gateway_grant` is what refuses a grant made to somebody else; it must be live and must **name the gateway being charged** (without that one check, one grant is a key to every gateway that lender owns); and the owner is **re-derived** — the platform owner tenant, or the lender's row read for that one id — so a ref carrying somebody else's tenant id changes nothing. **One door.** Every gateway vault read goes through `GatewayMerchant`, so "along a grant and nowhere else" is one file's property rather than four call sites' discipline: `credentialsFor`, `requireConfigured` and `configuredLabels` all route through the same `whereItLives`. A ref with no grant behaves exactly as before. **Audited by construction:** the vault writes its `tenant_credential_access` row inside the override, so it lands in the **lender's** scope, tagged `billing:<provider>:grant:<id>` — a lender can see every use of its own account and under which grant, and nothing here has to remember to log. Proof is deliberately split: `granted-vault-access.spec.ts` (9 cases) for the refusals, including that the override shuts again when the call throws — and it was mutation-checked, removing the wrong-gateway guard turns two of them red. `gateway-merchant.int.spec.ts` (4 new, 11 total) proves the crossing against the real policy, with the *same* borrower and credential that the file's existing case shows being refused **without** a grant; the log line names the grant and the lender's tenant. §8: `contract.vault.md` changed and its other consumers are `auth-service` and `bot-service` — **neither changes**, the override living in billing and the vault library being untouched. Billing unit 186 (9 new), billing int 68 (4 new), `npm test` 862 green, tsc clean over 16 tsconfigs. **Docs cap (§11):** `docs/domains/tenant/INDEX.md` lags — its `contract.vault.md` is at v8 with the crossing and the unit INDEX has no changelog row, billing's two files plus it being four. |
| F-096-d | A payment through a granted gateway accrues a debt to the borrowing tenant, in the settlement ledger, inside the crediting transaction | billing | done | F-096-c F-092-j | — | txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-settlement.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-settlement.spec.ts, docs/domains/billing/contract.deposit.md | Shipped 2026-09-12; the rules are `domains/billing/contract.deposit.md` "The debt it leaves", contract v13. **One place, because F-092-l already made it one place.** The accrual sits in `DepositSettlementService` beside the credit, so both ways a payment can be found to have landed — the payer's callback and the reconciliation sweep — accrue identically without either knowing it. **Inside the crediting transaction**, beside the ledger row and the outbox event: a wallet that grew without the debt recorded is a tenant owed money nothing knows about, and no later sweep can reconstruct it from a payment row. It hangs off the same `count` as the credit, so a retried callback accrues nothing, and F-096-a's unique key on `paymentTransactionId` is the second line under that. **Net of `feeApplied`** (§4): the payer covered the gateway's own cut and the gateway kept it, so it never reaches the owner's account and is not owed on. A fee larger than the credit floors at zero and logs — a negative accrual would be the platform quietly invoicing a tenant through a settlement ledger. Five new cases in a new `deposit-settlement.spec.ts`, which is also where the write *order* is asserted (flip, credit, confirm, accrue, announce) — mutation-checked: dropping the fee from the arithmetic turns two of them red. Five routes under `/api/billing/admin/settlement/*` — grant, withdraw, list, what is owed, record a payout. **Both things the row said to settle were decided with the user first, and neither was worked around.** (1) The RLS gap: the three tables key on the **borrowing** tenant, so they cannot express "only the platform owner may write". The answer is two doors — `settlement.manage` on the role, and `assertOperator` requiring `TenantType.platform_owner` — with the cross-tenant pool underneath, and **no database backstop**, stated as a limit in the contract rather than papered over with a `WITH CHECK` policy that would only restate the pool this service already chose. The permission is explicitly *not* the boundary: a reseller administers its own roles. That is invariant #9, and the spec asserts it over **every** public method from one list, so a method added later is covered without anyone remembering — dropping `assertOperator` from `owed()` alone turns two cases red. (2) `proofAttachmentKey` is a string the operator types, stored verbatim and resolved by nothing until F-033 lands D-8's port; the upload then writes the same column and no schema changes. **Where the code went, and the exception it opens:** `billing-service`, not beside the other `/admin` routes in `auth-service`, because the tables, the arithmetic and `isPlatformOwner` are all here — so this service becomes the second writer of `audit.admin_audit_log`, and `prisma/prisma.service.ts`'s "only ever queries the billing models" is amended with a pointer rather than quietly broken. Three `AdminAction` and two `AuditTargetType` values added (`20260912000300`) instead of reusing `tenant_settlement_approve`: a trail that cannot tell a grant from its withdrawal does not answer the question it exists for. **Refusals that are rules, not validation:** a gateway granted to the tenant that owns it (would double it on the top-up screen *and* accrue a debt from a tenant to itself), a second live grant of the same pair, a second withdrawal (the `updateMany` carries `isActive: true`, so a lost race writes no audit row — mutation-checked), and a payout above what is outstanding. What is owed is summed from the two ledgers every call, never cached; a tenant with a payout and no accrual shows negative rather than being hidden. 24 new cases in `settlement.service.spec.ts`, billing 191 -> 215, `npm test` **1841 green across all 7 projects**, `typecheck` clean over 16 tsconfigs. **The documented pair was not safe on this machine today** and that is worth the next session knowing: run together, `auth-service:test` took 3m7s on the critical path and four specs died on *timeouts* — billing's `rate-limit-coverage.spec.ts` at 30427ms against its own 30s budget, and three `otp.service.spec.ts` cases at ~5-6s against vitest's 5s default. Each passes alone in ~7s, and `npm test` alone is fully green, so it was contention and not a regression — but AGENTS.md's "132s for the pair, both fully green" did not hold here. **Caught by the 2s blast-radius grep, not the 180s pair:** the new rate-limit buckets broke `shared-core`'s `rate-limit-buckets.spec.ts` snapshot, which exists precisely so a new bucket is noticed — updated deliberately. int/e2e not run: no `contract.md` row changed and the schema move is enum values only. **Not verified against a live operator** — there is no admin UI, so these five routes have been proved by spec and typecheck and never by a person clicking them |

| F-092-k | Expire pending payments past `expiresAt` and release their coupon reservations | automation | done | F-092-j | — | txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-expiry.service.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-expiry.service.spec.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-internal.controller.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/jobs/deposit-expiry.job.ts, txnet-backend/shared-core/src/lib/http/service-only.guard.ts, docs/domains/billing/contract.deposit.md | Shipped 2026-09-12; the rules are `domains/billing/contract.deposit.md` "Expiring what nobody came back for", contract v8. **The clock is `automation`'s, the rule is `billing`'s.** `DepositExpiryJob` holds the tick and nothing else; what an expired payment *is* — the coupon SQL functions, the tenant-scoped pool and the cross-tenant one — stays in `billing-service` behind `POST /api/internal/billing/deposit/expire-pending`, the same seam `vault-retention.job.ts` established because an Nx app cannot import an Nx app. **The scan is cross-tenant and every write is not:** which tenants have a due payment is what the sweep is looking for, so the read cannot run inside one scope (and on the app pool RLS would answer zero rows), while the writes run one `tenantTransaction` per tenant because the coupon functions scope by exactly that binding. This is the second `CrossTenantPrismaService` reader in the service; the audit `grep` still lists them all. **The flip carries the callback's guard** — `updateMany({id, status: pending, expiresAt <= now})`, release hanging off its `count` — so a bank confirming between the scan and the write keeps its holds, and the sweep is safe to run twice as an at-least-once tick requires. Per payment rather than one batch update, because a count is not a list of which rows flipped. **Two deliberate differences from `close()`:** holds are released `expired`, not `cancelled` (nothing failed, the clock ran out), and `expiresAt` is **not** cleared — F-092-l inquires an expired payment later and when we stopped waiting is part of judging a mismatch. Legacy deleted the row and its locks on two separate Mongo TTLs, which is how a lock could outlive its payment and an abandoned attempt left no trace. **Raised, not folded in:** billing needed `ServiceOnlyGuard` and there were already two copies (auth's reads a middleware flag; bot's is self-contained). It went to `shared-core` with one consumer rather than being spelled a third time — `bot-service`'s copy should collapse into it, and doing that here would be editing a service this row has no business in. Edges the row implied but did not name, all in `app.module.ts` and its spec: `internal/*` is excluded from `IdentityMiddleware` (a process carries no `X-User-Id` and no tenant), `DepositInternalController` is named in `rate-limit-coverage.spec.ts`'s `EXEMPT` with the reason, and `SERVICE_AUTH_TOKEN` is now required in billing when `NODE_ENV=production` — empty closes the seam, which is safe but silent. Traefik needed nothing: `/api/internal` is not under the `/api/billing` router. **It will not run until an operator schedules it** (`/admin/workers`, F-031-b) — a registered job with no `bot_schedule` row ticks never, which is the deliberate default for a sweep that writes and the first thing to check; `SURFACES.md` carries that as a flow row. Replaces the legacy Mongo TTL indexes on Transactions and CouponLocks, which deleted the pending row and its audit trail with it. A worker-service job shaped like vault-retention.job.ts |
| F-092-l | Reconciliation: inquire a pending or expired payment at the gateway; paid → `reconciliation_auto` credit through the same guarded path, amount mismatch → `flagged_mismatch` | automation | done | F-092-j | — | txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-reconciliation.service.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-reconciliation.service.spec.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-settlement.ts, txnet-backend/billing-service/src/app/payment/deposit/deposit-internal.controller.ts, txnet-backend/worker-service/src/app/jobs/deposit-reconciliation.job.ts, docs/domains/billing/contract.deposit.md | Shipped 2026-09-12; the rules are `domains/billing/contract.deposit.md` "Asking the gateway later", contract v9. **This row exists because F-092-j refuses to guess.** A verify that timed out, an unreadable merchant id, a bank that said nothing — all of them leave the payment `pending`, which is only correct if something later goes and asks. Legacy's `isVerified` (100/101) was the same idea with nothing scheduling it. **It credits and it flags; it never closes and it never reverses** (invariant 9). `ReconciliationAction`'s three words are the whole vocabulary: a `verified`/`paid` inquiry is followed by a real `verify` (that call is where the reference number is) and credited `reconciliation_auto`; an `amount_mismatch` is `flagged_mismatch` and nothing else happens, because crediting either figure would be the job inventing a price; `in_bank`, `failed` and `reversed` are recorded and acted on not at all — closing a row is the clock's job and reversing one is nobody's. **An answer the gateway could not give writes no log row at all**, which is the load-bearing detail: a row saying "checked, nothing to do" is what retires a payment from every later sweep, and a timeout is not an answer. `authority_invalid` is the one exception, being final. The recheck and lookback windows are what stop the oldest unresolvable payment filling every batch for ever. **One refactor, made because the second caller arrived:** the flip + credit + coupon confirm + outbox event moved out of `deposit-callback.service.ts` into `DepositSettlementService`, so the two ways of learning a payment landed cannot become two ways of crediting it. Behaviour is unchanged and that is not an assertion — the callback's own 12 specs run against the real settlement service over the same fakes, and passed unmodified except for the constructor line. Raised, not built: nothing surfaces a `flagged_mismatch` to an operator (it is a table and a `bot_execution_log` metric), and `payment_reconciliation_log` carries no `tenantId`, so it is the one billing table with no RLS policy of its own — both are rows of their own, both written into the contract. **Like every job it ticks only once an operator schedules it** (`/admin/workers`). A rejected code stays in the list marked, carrying billing's own translated sentence; this app branches on no reason code. **The free path finishes on this page**, because `start` credits inside its own transaction and mints nothing to come back from — F-093-f's pages only see a bank's redirect. | F-093-f | Payment result pages: success (reference copy, already paid) and failure (error code → message) | panel-web | done | F-093-a F-092-j | — | site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/payment/_lib/payment-result.ts, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/payment/_lib/payment-result.test.ts, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/payment/success/page.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/payment/failed/page.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/payment/_components/PaymentResultCard.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/payment/_components/PaymentSuccessView.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/payment/_components/PaymentFailedView.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/payment/_components/ReferenceCard.tsx, site-pwa/src/lib/routes.ts, site-pwa/src/generated/i18n-keys.ts, locales/frontend/langs/fa/common.json, locales/frontend/langs/en/common.json, docs/interfaces/panel-web/contract.payment-result.md | Shipped 2026-09-12; the rules are `panel-web/contract.payment-result.md`, unit v17. **These pages report an outcome that is already final** — the callback verified, flipped and credited before the redirect, so there is no call, no retry and no balance arithmetic here, and the whole of both pages is how three query parameters are read. Server-read `searchParams` rather than `useSearchParams`, so legacy's `Suspense` fallback is not the first thing a paying user sees. Two couplings are **checked rather than copied**: `payment-result.test.ts` reads `CallbackFailureCode` out of `deposit-callback.service.ts` and `RESULT_PATH` out of the controller, so a code or a path renamed on billing's side fails here instead of rendering a blank card or a 404. Three legacy behaviours dropped on purpose: a missing `?ref=` was shown as *payment failed* when the bank had confirmed it; the raw `?error=` was echoed as `Error Code: …`, which is a stranger's text under our branding on the page a user is most willing to act on, so an unrecognised code now gets the general sentence and is not printed; and the support link pointed at a page that does not exist (`support` is `href: null`). **Known gap, not a blocker:** the pages sit inside `(panel)`, so a session that expired during the trip to the bank is a login redirect that loses the query string — the payment itself settled before the redirect and the reference is on `/financial`. site-pwa 494 tests (17 new, red before `payment-result.ts` existed), tsc clean over the app, eslint clean; no backend TypeScript touched, so the workspace pair was not run; no int/e2e run. | F-093-g | Gift code modal | panel-web | done | F-093-c F-092-m | — | site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/_components/GiftCodeModal.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/_components/GiftCodeModal.test.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/_components/WalletButton.tsx, site-pwa/src/lib/billing-api.ts, site-pwa/src/generated/i18n-keys.ts, locales/frontend/langs/fa/common.json, locales/frontend/langs/en/common.json | Shipped 2026-09-12; the rules are `panel-web/contract.shell.md` "The gift-code modal". **The bug is gone in both halves:** a refusal is an `ApiError` with no success path below it, and a redemption makes the top bar *re-read* rather than add an amount — legacy did `walletBalance + data.amount` on every answer, `undefined` on a refusal, so a dead code showed success over `NaN`. The nine specs exist for that one invariant. The five refusal sentences stay `billing`'s: each already names the box the code belongs in (a discount code is told to use top-up), so this app copies none of them and branches on no reason code. A quick action's destination is now an `href` **or** a `modal` — the first overlay the wallet opens rather than navigates to; it renders as a `button`, and top-up alone is still hidden pending F-093-e. Legacy's self-dismiss timer is not ported: its callback read a stale `status` to decide whether to fire. **Found, not fixed:** `contract.gift.md` says a refusal carries a machine-readable `reason` beside the `i18nKey`, and the shared `I18nExceptionFilter` does not forward it — the envelope is `{ok, msg, ref, fieldErrors}`. Nothing here needs it, since each message is a complete sentence, so it is raised rather than built: adding a field to the envelope is every service's change, not this row's. **Polish pass 2026-09-12, at the user's ask:** the modal is the most animated surface in the panel now — spring entrance, a check that draws itself, a spark burst, a shake on a refusal, legacy's two glow orbs brought back. All of it is on the frame and none on the money (no figure is animated between values), all of it collapses under `prefers-reduced-motion`, and every decorative element is inert and `aria-hidden`. It also locks the page scroll while open. **Measured, and it changed the design:** the code box is controlled, so every keystroke re-renders the dialog and each framer-motion component in it pays for that. Six of them took the slowest spec from 2.0s to 4.2s against vitest's 5s ceiling — it then failed 4 of 6 full-suite runs while passing alone, a timeout that reads exactly like a broken assertion. Three things fixed it: one-shot entrances and the infinite orb pulse moved to CSS (`.gift-rise`, `.gift-pop`, `.gift-orb` in `globals.css`); the specs fill the box with one change event instead of eight keystrokes where the typing is not the point; and the file then takes `testTimeout: 15s`, because the residue was contention from the other 19 spec files, not this code — a timeout that reads exactly like a broken assertion. 4.2s -> 1.7s alone, and **5 consecutive clean full runs** where 4 of 6 had been failing. framer-motion is kept only for an exit, an `auto` height and the imperative shake; `contract.shell.md` rule 6 is that rule with its number. **Opened invisible, and the cause was not where it looked.** The modal is portaled to `document.body` now: its caller sits in a top bar with `backdrop-blur-xl`, and an element with a `backdrop-filter` is the containing block for fixed-position descendants — so `fixed inset-0` meant the 64px bar, not the viewport, and the bar's `z-20` capped the stacking order so `z-50` never cleared the sidebar's `z-40`. Reads as `overflow: hidden` on the nav and is not. `contract.shell.md` rule 9 flags it for F-093-h's notifications panel, which will hit both. **Three faults the user found in `fa`, all fixed:** the modal was centred in a frame with no scroll, so an error message grew the card and clipped its top off screen — the overlay is `overflow-y-auto` + `min-h-full` now, with the backdrop `fixed` and a heavier blur. The focus underline under the code box mixed a logical `start-1/2` with a physical `-translate-x-1/2` and ran off the left edge in RTL; it is removed, since the border and glow already mark focus. The success burst had the same mix and is physical on both sides now. `contract.shell.md` rule 8 plus the RTL-trap note is that class of bug written down. Also caught on the way: `primary-dark` is not a token in this app's Tailwind v4 `@theme` (only `--color-primary` and `--color-primary-glow` are), so the three gradients that used it were rendering to a dead colour — they are `from-primary to-primary/70` now, which holds in all three themes. site-pwa 458 tests (10 new), tsc clean; no backend TS touched, so the workspace pair was not run; no int/e2e run. Not verified in a browser — no running stack this session |
| F-093-i | Return-to-intent on the login redirect: the panel guard remembers the path it bounced from and restores it after sign-in, relative paths only | panel-web | done | F-093-f | — | site-pwa/src/lib/return-to.ts, site-pwa/src/lib/return-to.test.ts, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/_context/PanelSessionContext.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/_context/PanelRealtimeContext.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/_components/LogoutButton.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(panel)/_components/AccountSwitcher.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(auth)/auth/login/page.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(auth)/auth/register/page.tsx, site-pwa/src/app/(auth)/auth/forgot-password/page.tsx, docs/architecture/decisions/0042-a-bounced-deep-link-is-remembered-as-a-relative-path-in-the-tab.md | Shipped 2026-09-12; the rules are `panel-web/contract.session-guard.md` "Return-to-intent on the login redirect", unit v18, and **ADR-0042**, which the row asked for and which is where the two *whys* live. **One module, called from the guards.** `lib/return-to.ts` owns the validation, the store and the consumption; the four bounce sites call `rememberReturnTo` and the three sign-in screens call `consumeReturnTo`. No page participates and none changed — the destination is lost *at the guard*, so a per-page fix would be the same rule written five times and forgotten in the sixth. **The validation is the feature.** A stored destination is a relative path and nothing else — one leading `/`, never `//`, no backslash (browsers fold it into a slash, so `/\evil` is `//evil` when navigated), no whitespace or control characters — checked on the way in *and* on the way out, and `/auth/*` refused as a loop. Anything failing is dropped for the panel home rather than corrected. The spec spends a case on each, because each is a separate way a validator gets written wrongly. **`sessionStorage`, not `?returnTo=`:** the redirect is client-side and in the same tab, so a store suffices; it keeps a payment reference out of a second URL, out of history and out of logs; and it leaves nothing an outside link can forge, which a query parameter is by construction. **Found while wiring it, and handled:** the bounce happens in *four* places, not the one the row named — `PanelSessionContext` plus both of `PanelRealtimeContext`'s session-lost paths; and the two **deliberate** sign-outs (`LogoutButton`, the switcher's *sign out of all devices*) must **clear** rather than remember, or the next person to sign in on that tab lands on the previous one's payment. **Raised, not fixed** (in the ADR's consequences and the contract): `src/proxy.ts`'s server-side auth-screen guard cannot participate — it runs before any script and cannot read the tab's storage — so a signed-in visitor opening `/auth/login` still goes to the panel home with a stored intent left behind. Bounded: the next bounce overwrites it, a sign-out clears it, and making it participate means putting the path back in the URL, which is what ADR-0042 rule 1 refuses. site-pwa 503 tests (9 new, red before the module existed), tsc clean over the app, eslint unchanged (its two findings in these files are pre-existing and identical before and after). No backend TypeScript touched, so the workspace pair was not run; no int/e2e run. Not verified in a browser — no running stack this session. |

Legacy's component rendered dummy data, so what ported is its shape — bell, badge, typed rows, empty state — and not its code. **The badge is `notification-service`'s `unreadCount`, never counted from the ten rows the dropdown reads**, and a mark-read takes the new count from that write's own answer; this is `useWalletBalance`'s rule on a different figure. It re-reads on `notification.created` alone, unlike the wallet which re-reads on any event — the shape is agreed here (`RealtimeEvents`, C-08) and the `user:` channel also carries the payment events of F-067-l/m. **The bar had no room and that was an architectural question, asked before it was built** (user, 2026-09-20): `contract.shell.md` rule 5 measured 304px of content box against ~286px used, and a `p-2` bell is 40px. Opening the panel marks nothing read, on purpose. No "see all" footer — legacy's led nowhere and no inbox page exists; that row fills it in.
