## Order of work — the F-027 network metering series

Opened 2026-09-21 from the architecture plan, decided by **ADR-0071** (the
network plane is its own Go deployable), **ADR-0072** (traffic is paid for
before it is served), **ADR-0073** (a metered rate is locked on the Grant),
**ADR-0074** (a panel declares its counter semantics and transport) and
**ADR-0075** (an exhausted Grant suspends, and its clients are purged on a
clock). It replaces the umbrella rows F-027 and F-028.

The order is not a preference. Three places in it are load-bearing:

- **F-027-j before any real driver.** The fake driver and its conformance suite
  are the only way to build and prove six families without owning a server.
- **F-027-s and F-027-t before F-027-u.** The ceiling is what makes the loss
  zero; the hot loop only makes it comfortable. Shipping the loop first means
  running with the window open for longer.
- **F-027-v with F-027-u, never after.** An unbudgeted hot loop is a denial of
  service against a customer's own server.

In order:

1. Schema — `F-027-a` → `F-027-b` → `F-027-c` → `F-027-d` → `F-027-e`;
   `F-027-f` → `F-027-g` (independent of the network tables, may run alongside);
   `F-027-ak` repairs what `F-027-e` left open and is a dependency of `F-027-n`
2. Go service and drivers — `F-027-h` → `F-027-i` → `F-027-j` → `F-027-k`
3. Collection, no money — `F-027-l` → `F-027-m` → `F-027-n` → `F-027-o`
4. Money — `F-027-p` → `F-027-q` → `F-027-r`
5. The guarantee — `F-027-s` → `F-027-t`
6. Speed and safety — `F-027-u` → `F-027-v` → `F-027-w`
7. Lifecycle — `F-027-x` → `F-027-y`
8. Convergence and drift — `F-027-z` → `F-027-aa` → `F-027-ab`
9. Surfaces — `F-027-ac`; `F-027-aq` → `F-027-ar` → (`F-027-as`, `F-027-at`) → `F-027-ad` (ADR-0080)
10. Real drivers — `F-027-ae` → `F-027-af` → `F-027-ag` → `F-027-ah` →
    `F-027-ai`; `F-027-ae` → one row per pull family (`F-027-ba`..`F-027-be`), then `F-027-bg` (client path); `F-027-ae` → `F-027-aw` (the Opener) → `F-027-ax` (the store)

11. The lease planner (ADR-0093, from `quotaengine`) — `F-027-cw` → `F-027-cx`
    → `F-027-cy` → `F-027-cz` → `F-027-da` → **`F-027-db` (cutover)** →
    (`F-027-dc`, `F-027-dd`, `F-027-de` → `F-027-df`, `F-027-dg`, `F-027-dh`,
    `F-027-dj`, `F-027-dm`, `F-027-dn`) → `F-027-dk`. `F-027-di` and `F-027-dl` need only
    `F-027-cw` / nothing. The shadow rows come first because two writers of one
    ceiling on a panel fight: nothing before `F-027-db` writes a ceiling.

Answered since the series opened:

- **Mikrotik WireGuard is cancelled** (user, 2026-09-21). RouterOS has no
  per-peer quota, so it cannot hold a ceiling and cannot sell metered service.
  `F-027-aj` is `dropped`. A RouterOS-queue fence would be a new row.
- **The original x-ui (vaxilu) is set aside** (user, 2026-09-24). It meters
  per inbound, not per client, and one inbound per config was declined.
  `F-027-bd` is `dropped`; the `x_ui_vaxilu` enum value stays.
- **Cloudius is set aside** (user, 2026-09-24). No API docs or real install
  exist for this system, and a driver built on guessed wire shapes would pass
  its own suite. `F-027-ai` is `dropped`; the `cloudius` enum value stays.
- **Tax moved to the top-up** (user, 2026-09-21) — **ADR-0076**, superseding
  ADR-0038. It is not in this series: it belongs to the gateway path, as
  `F-104-ae` → `F-104-af` → `F-104-ag` → `F-104-ah`. The two are connected:
  taxing the sale is what ADR-0072 made impractical, because a metered sale is
  a 4c block bought many times a day. **Nothing in this series charges tax.**

Still open — see plan section 23:

- The ceiling horizon safety factor starts at 4 and is a setting, not a
  constant, so it can be tuned once real data exists.

## Order of work — the F-067 queue series

Sequenced 2026-09-10 on the user's call: **every queue row ships before the
WebSocket work starts.** The two phases are genuinely separable — only F-067-j
crosses the line, which is why F-067-a was split.

**Phase 1 — the queue.** Run these with `/next <id>`, one per session:

1. `F-067-d` dead-letter path. First, because every other row puts a message on
   the broker that does not recur, and today a failed handler destroys it.
2. `F-067-g` monitoring. Immediately after, not later: a dead-letter queue
   nobody watches loses messages more slowly, not less often.
3. `F-067-f` publisher confirms. Small, and F-067-a is unsafe without it.
4. `F-067-a` OTP leaves the request path. The first row a user feels, and the
   one that stops a slow SMS provider from degrading `/auth/login`.
5. `F-067-b` the bot webhook acks and enqueues.
6. `F-067-c` outbox + relay. Cheap now and expensive on the day `billing` needs
   it; nothing consumes it before then, so it waits for the rows above.
7. `F-067-e` the tenant cap on a shared counter. Last on purpose — no publisher
   sets `tenantId`, so the gate sees no traffic to be wrong about yet. **Done
   2026-09-10; Phase 1 is complete.**

**Phase 2 — the socket.** ~~`F-067-h` (the gateway)~~ and ~~`F-067-i` (the
fan-out to the replica holding the connection)~~ **both done 2026-09-10**, then
~~`F-067-j` (the OTP result stops being polled)~~ **done 2026-09-10**, together
with `F-067-k` — the anonymous socket it turned out to need (ADR-0031), built
in the same session on the user's call rather than deferred to its own.
`F-034` live chat follows on the same set. **The series is complete on the
server side.**

**Phase 3 — the client.** Added 2026-09-10: every row above shipped without a
browser opening the socket, so the whole transport currently has no consumer.
`F-070-a` (the client) first — it is the only one of the three that the other
two both need. `F-070-b` (the auth screens) second, because it is the half a
user actually feels and F-067-j is inert until it lands. ~~`F-070-c` (the
signed-in socket) last, and possibly not at all before `F-034`~~ — **all three
done 2026-09-10**; F-070-c was built on the user's call rather than deferred,
so `F-034` starts from a socket that is already held. Nothing publishes to a
signed-in channel yet, which is why its proof is a connection that survives
navigation and a switch.

Two notes for whoever plans the sessions:

- ~~**F-067-e and F-067-i both want a Redis in `worker-service`,**~~ — settled
  2026-09-10: F-067-e landed first and paid for it. `worker-service` now has a
  `RedisService` and a `redis.keys.ts` of its own, so F-067-i's fan-out starts
  from a client that exists.
- ~~**Phase 1 is seven sessions, Phase 2 is three plus a unit that does not
  exist yet.**~~ — settled 2026-09-10: `realtime` was created at F-067-h on the
  user's word, deployed as `gateway-service`. ~~**F-067-i is now the row that
  gates production**~~ — **done 2026-09-10**: the fan-out lands, so
  `gateway-service` may be scaled past one replica. F-067-j is the only row
  left in the series.

## Order of work — the F-071 hardcoding series

Opened 2026-09-11 from the user's question: *if we change a password or a key,
do we have to hunt through the whole codebase?* An inventory across Go,
TypeScript and YAML said yes. **ADR-0036 is the decision these rows implement —
read it once, and no row needs this plan re-explained to it.** Each row below is
one session inside the v2.8 caps and carries its own file list in its `note`.

Three tracks. They are independent of each other, so a session can take the head
of any of them, but **within** a track the order is load-bearing.

**Track 1 — the wire contracts.** Cheapest, and it is where the drift is already
visible.

1. `F-071` headers get one home and a Go/TS fixture. First, because it proves
   the fixture pattern that Track 2 leans on.
2. `F-072` `tools/contracts.py` holds the Traefik lists to it.
3. `F-073` the refresh cookie. This one closes a live bug.
4. `F-074` the remaining headers import the constants that already exist.

**Track 2 — the Redis keyspace.** Riskiest. Deliberately *after* `F-071`: the
keyspace change is the one that can log every user out, and it should not also
be the session that debugs a new fixture harness.

5. `F-075` the prefix gets one home and one value (`v2`). **Read its note before
   starting** — there is a scan to run first, and it can stop the row.
6. `F-076` the four builders collapse into one, and Go gets a builder at all.
7. `F-077` rate-limit buckets become a typed registry.
8. `F-078` every lifetime is a `RedisTtl` entry.

**Track 3 — i18n.** Largest, and the only one that adds a build step, a
committed generated artifact and a CI job. `F-080` gates everything after it:
generate before migrating, always.

9. `F-080` the generators read `locales/` and their output is committed.
10. `F-081` the five keys spelled in both Go and TypeScript.
11. `F-082` then `F-083` the panel namespaces.
12. `F-084` the dynamic families, which generated types cannot reach.

**Loose rows, any gap:** `F-079` (AMQP exchange), `F-086` (Go boot timeout),
`F-088` (panel build literals), `F-089` (`billing-service` env schema).
`F-085` needs `F-072` first. `F-087` is `needs-decision` — its note holds the
question.

## Order of work — the legacy port series

Opened 2026-09-11 from the user's request: the previous Next.js + MongoDB app
already built rial payments, coupons, a wallet page and a dashboard. **Reuse
its logic and its UI; change no architecture.** A `legacy:` path in a note is a
path under `docs/legacy/txnetsite-perv/`, where the parts worth porting were
extracted once. **Read its `README.md` table and open only your row's files** —
never the folder whole, never the zip. Never port a legacy file
as is: Mongo becomes Prisma, integer rial becomes USD `Decimal` (ADR-0019), a
Persian literal becomes an i18n key, a plaintext merchant id becomes a Vault
read. Each note names the legacy bug that must not come across.

Three tracks:

**Track A — can start today, UI only.** `F-093-a` shell, then `F-093-b` kit. Both
render without billing data.

**Track B — the money path.** D-19..D-21 answered 2026-09-11. **The path now waits on F-025's FX
worker** (D-19), which only `F-092-c` and `F-092-i` need — everything before
them can be built first. In order: `F-094` → `F-092-a` → `F-095` → `F-092-b` → `F-092-d` → `F-092-e` → `F-092-q` → `F-092-f`
→ `F-092-g` → `F-092-h` → `F-092-o` → `F-092-c` → `F-092-i` → `F-092-j`, and
after it `F-092-k`, `F-092-l` in either order. `F-092-m`, `F-092-n`, `F-092-p` any time
after `F-092-b`.

**Track C — the pages,** each as soon as its billing row is done: `F-093-e`
after `F-092-i`, `F-093-f` after `F-092-j`. `F-093-h` waits for F-035-a and F-035-b. `F-093-i` came out of `F-093-f` and needs nothing: it is a guard row, not a page.
~~`F-093-c`~~, ~~`F-093-d`~~ and ~~`F-093-g`~~ are **done 2026-09-12**. `F-093-c`
only needed `F-092-n` for the balance, not `F-092-m`, and it settled the panel ->
billing data path the rest of Track C now reuses. `F-093-e` is the last quick
action still hidden in the wallet dropdown, so it is the one that finishes that
control.

Not ported, because this repo already has it: login / signup / OTP routes,
AuthForm, NatureCaptcha, lib/auth, session, captcha, rateLimiter, sms, rbac,
permissions, proxy.ts, the Users model (F-001..F-016, F-0201, F-009, F-007,
F-008, F-053); ThemeToggle and Toaster (the panel has its own). Bot rows F-304
and F-306 reuse `F-092-i`, `F-092-j` and `F-092-n` instead of a second payment
path (ADR-0009).

## Order of work — the F-104 gateway series

Opened 2026-09-14 (D-32): Stripe, NOWPayments, OxaPay, Airwallex, Telegram Stars
and Bale's in-chat payment. **None of these needs a live account to reach
`done`**: a driver is proved by fixtures and signature vectors taken from the
provider's own docs. A sandbox run, and then one small real payment through the
test mode (a gateway that is off or unverified, visible only to its admin), is
the manual check before a person marks the gateway verified.

In order: `F-104-a` → `F-104-b` (with its ADR) → `F-104-c` → `F-104-e` →
`F-104-f` → `F-104-g` (Stripe proves the webhook door) → `F-104-d` →
`F-104-h`, `F-104-i` in either order → `F-104-j`. The bot track can start once
`F-104-e` is done: `F-306-a` and `F-104-l` in either order → `F-104-k` →
`F-104-m` → `F-104-n` → `F-104-q` → `F-104-o`. `F-104-p` any time after `F-104-a`.

Review fixes (2026-09-16), each independent: money first — `F-104-r`, `F-104-s`,
`F-104-t` — then `F-104-v`, `F-104-u`, `F-104-w`, `F-104-y`, `F-104-z`, `F-104-aa`.
`F-104-x` was answered and built 2026-09-20.

## Order of work — the F-502 coupon series

Opened 2026-09-14 (D-33): managing coupons and gift codes. Using them already
ships (F-092-g/h/m, F-093-e/g).

In order: `F-502-a` → `F-502-b` → `F-502-j` → `F-502-k` → `F-502-c` →
`F-502-d`, `F-502-e` in either order → `F-502-f` → `F-502-g` → `F-502-h`,
`F-502-i` in either order. `F-502-l-a` → `F-502-l-b` → `F-502-l-c` (D-35).

Review fixes (2026-09-16), each independent: `F-502-m` first (a key lost for
good), then `F-093-q`, `F-093-r`, `F-093-s`, `F-093-t`, `F-096-f`, `F-096-g`,
`F-502-n`, `F-502-o`. The same review's `start` finding is `F-104-w`.

`F-502-m` shipped the lock but not the way out: `F-502-p` (the reissue
route) then `F-502-q` (the panel button) are what make a lost key
recoverable, and rule 11 of `panel-web/contract.gift-code.md` is written to
be relaxed when they land. Opened 2026-09-20 with the user, unstarted.

## Order of work — the F-026 catalog and Grant series

Opened 2026-09-14 (D-34, ADR-0049): the spec's product model and Grant, both as
modules inside `billing-service`, to unblock `F-502-l`. No purchase flow and no
provisioning in this series.

In order: `F-026-a` → `F-026-b` → `F-026-c` → `F-026-e`, `F-026-d` in either
order → `F-026-f`. `F-502-l` may start as soon as `F-026-e` is done.

## Order of work — the F-1533 catalog translation series

Opened 2026-09-16 (D-36, ADR-0050): product and category names in every
language — `fa`/`en` written by the admin, the rest drafted by a local engine
and published by a human. No languages are added by this series.

In order: `F-1533-a` and `F-1533-b` in either order → `F-1533-c` →
`F-1533-d` → `F-1533-e`.

## Order of work — the F-601 retention notices series

Opened 2026-09-27 (user). `F-601-a` first: every other row only emits.
Then, by the user's priority: `F-601-c` → `F-601-d` → `F-601-e` →
`F-601-b`. The rest in any order after their dependencies; `F-603` waits on
its decision, `F-1515` on the ai unit.

## Rules

- Never delete a row. Move it to `dropped` with a reason in `note`.
- An item too big to finish in one session must be split into sub-items first.
- `blocked` requires the blocker named in `note` and a matching entry in the
  unit's `open-questions.md`.
- Ids are permanent. Never renumber.

## Decisions needed from the user

Items flagged `needs-decision` — the loop will not touch these until answered.

**Every decision below is answered.** D-19..D-27 (legacy port series) were asked and answered on 2026-09-11. The table is kept as the
record of what was asked and where the answer now lives. A `needs-decision` flag
still on a row means something else — almost always a catalog spec that has not
been ingested yet — not one of these.

No ADR — §6.6: the status-quo option beating an obvious rival is a note, and the note is on F-039 |
| D-3 | Cross-domain event transport: synchronous calls, transactional outbox, or RabbitMQ? — blocked F-021, F-027, F-035, F-038 | 2026-09-04 | 2026-09-09 | **Transactional outbox, relayed to RabbitMQ** — ADR-0021. Brings a new unit with it: the outbox table, the relay, and consumer idempotency |
| D-4 | Credential encryption mechanism (KMS / app-level key / cipher) for tenant + Panel secrets — blocked F-018, F-027 | 2026-09-04 | 2026-09-09 | **AES-256-GCM in the application, key from a Docker Swarm secret, versioned per ciphertext** — ADR-0022 |

Blocked F-028, F-041 | 2026-09-04 | 2026-09-09 | **Hand-written `.sql` in the same `prisma/domains/migrations/` history, applied before the first production data** — `docs/operations/migrations.md` |

| D-37 | On an admin surface over a shape-B RLS table, which pool serves which caller | 2026-09-17 | 2026-09-17 | **"C": a tenant admin on the app pool (`withTenant` + RLS), only the platform owner on the cross-tenant pool; one `access()` decides, a spec asserts a non-owner never touches the cross-tenant pool** — the user's call over A (all cross-tenant, as coupons) and B (relax the policy). ADR-0053; row F-035-c, billing follow-up F-102-f |
| D-38 | Campaign SMS: whose line, who pays, who may send | 2026-09-17 | 2026-09-17 | **"C", staged: the platform's line, unmetered, carries only the platform owner's own campaign to its own verified phones; a reseller's SMS draft is refused (409 `sms_not_available`); one resolver (`sms-line.ts`) decides, so F-035-i (own line / metering, after F-018) changes one function** — the user's call over A (platform line for all, unbilled) and B (full model now, blocked on F-018). Row F-035-f |
| D-39 | A user's email: which provider sends, how ownership is proven, which path the send takes | 2026-09-17 | 2026-09-17 | **Plain SMTP (nodemailer, `MailProviderService` in `shared-core`; Mailpit in dev); a six-digit emailed code, not a link; sent through the OTP worker path (F-067-a), `email` an `OtpChannel` that only `email_verify` may use** — the user's call over an HTTP API vendor / a stub, a signed link, and an inline send. Row F-035-g; panel split to F-035-j |
| D-40 | Campaign email: whose sender, where the subject comes from, whether campaigns are translated | 2026-09-17 | 2026-09-17 | **Email on D-38's terms (the platform owner's own campaign to its own verified addresses; 409 `email_not_available` otherwise). An editable subject with a translated default, and the whole campaign machine-drafted into every language and published by an admin, on every channel, in this row; texts in a `notification` table, not locale-service** — the user's calls. ADR-0055. Row F-035-h |
| D-41 | F-019: how a reseller pays the platform, and whether a reseller may use the platform's SMS line | 2026-09-17 | 2026-09-17 | **Prepaid wallet only; top-up through the platform owner's existing gateways; subscription with 3 days' grace then suspension, no data deleted. The platform's SMS line serves only the platform's own sends; a tenant sends SMS on its own line and number, like its own gateway, so there is no SMS metering or price**: SMS price varies by number and text and changes over time, and not every provider reports it. `use_platform_sms` / `sms_sent` / `sms_usage_charge` stay in the schema unused. OTP SMS: only a reseller's owner gets it on the platform's line; its staff and users on the reseller's own line, or `sms` is not offered (row F-018-b). Rows F-019-a..d; F-035-i-b dropped |
| D-42 | F-018: what a suspended tenant keeps, one RBAC or two for reseller staff, where uploaded branding lives, how much onboarding ships first | 2026-09-17 | 2026-09-17 | **(1) Staged suspension** in one `TenantStatusPolicy` matrix, `/sub` held 7 days (a setting), nothing deleted — F-018-f. **(2) One RBAC:** `identity.Role` scoped per tenant, staff membership only in `tenant_staff_member` — F-018-n, F-018-j. **(3) A storage port with a local driver now, S3 later without a migration of rows** — keys not URLs, one serving route — F-018-m (new platform unit `object-storage`), F-018-h. **(4) The onboarding gate + a computed checklist first**, import later — F-018-l. All four as the agent recommended; (3) changed by the user to local storage first |
| D-43 | F-114-e: what replaces the "subscription key" a user is shown once (D-35, F-502-p) | 2026-09-25 | 2026-09-25 | **The `/sub` link, always available in My services; the word "key" is gone** — the user's call, as recommended, after an example (a VPN app cannot sign in, so the link carries a secret). The token is kept sealed as well as hashed; reset is for a leaked link only. ADR-0085; rows F-114-e-a..c |
| D-44 | F-114-f: where the list of product capabilities comes from | 2026-09-25 | 2026-09-25 | **A fixed platform list plus each tenant's own capabilities, in a catalog table with translated names; billing refuses a key not on the list; keys already in use are migrated in** — the user's call over the agent's recommendation (a fixed list only), then rows before code, as recommended. ADR-0086; rows F-114-f-a..c |
| D-45 | F-114-h: where a discount without a code is computed | 2026-09-25 | 2026-09-25 | **Two layers.** A time-boxed campaign and seasonal pricing (F-503, F-505) are new catalog price rows with `effectiveFrom`, never an overwrite; a targeted discount (category, group, named users, volume F-504) is a billing discount rule applied at invoice pricing beside coupons — the agent's recommendation, taken by the user. ADR written with F-114-h. |
| D-46 | F-111-u: where a config's line comes from when the panel's sub server gives none | 2026-09-26 | 2026-09-26 | **Built by the driver from the inbound's settings, a port of x-ui's `inbound.js`; the sub server is not read; never a guessed line; an empty capture is asked again** — the user's call (2026-09-26), over the agent's recommendation to read the sub server first and build only as a fallback. ADR-0088 |
| D-47 | F-307-f..k: how a config is named in the buyer's app, and where the default name lives | 2026-09-26 | 2026-09-26 | **Display only, named where served (billing's list and `/sub` alike); order: buyer's label, tenant template, platform `{region}`, panel name; the default is a template per request, owned by the tenant** — the agent's recommendation, accepted by the user. [ADR-0089](architecture/decisions/0089-a-config-line-is-named-when-it-is-served.md) |
| D-48 | Same panel registered twice (another domain); one panel selling different inbounds per group | 2026-09-26 | 2026-09-26 | **Registered once, identified by what the panel answers (normalised address, `claimTag`, canary); selling settings resolve member -> panel -> platform, an inbound is the pool's or one group's** — ADR-0090, rows F-027-cd..ci |
| D-49 | How a Grant's bag is split between its idle configs (50 GB on two inbounds read 49.9 / 0.09) | 2026-09-26 | 2026-09-26 | **Floor in seconds of the config's panel line (180 s), at least 100 MiB, at most an even share; no line rate = even share; the rest to the hot config** — delegated to the agent by the user. ADR-0091 |

Decided in the same session, though never a `D-` row: **a request resolves its
tenant from the Host header, falling back to a per-deployment default tenant**
(ADR-0020). That is F-061, which unblocks the second half of F-059.
